```latex

Next compute $g_0=(b_0,\dots,b_{n-1})$ by solving a linear program, i.e.\ maximizing $\sum_j b_j$ subject to $b_j\ge0$ and $\|f*g_0\|_\infty\le M$; as is standard, the optimum is attained at an extreme point determined by an active set of binding inequalities, here corresponding to important constraints where the convolution bound $(f*g_0)(x)\le M$ is tight and limiting. Rescale $g_0$ to match the normalization, $g=\frac{\sqrt{2n}}{\sum_j b_j}g_0$, and update $f\leftarrow (1-t)f+t g$ for a small $t>0$. Repeating this step produces a sequence with nonincreasing $\|f*f\|_\infty$, and the iteration is continued until it stabilizes.
```"

Let's write functions:

```
import numpy as np
import time
import math

def finite_float_list(seq):
    # convert to plain float list nonnegative finite? for final maybe
    out=[]
    for x in seq:
       xf=float(x)
       if not math.isfinite(xf): return None
       if xf < 0: return None
       if xf > 1000: xf=1000.0
       out.append(xf)
    if len(out)==0: return None
    s=sum(out)
    if s < 0.01: return None
    return out
```
But helper no closures. okay.

`fast_exact_score(a)`:
```
def fast_exact_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return np.inf
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01: return np.inf
    ss = float(np.dot(a,a))
    # max convolution is at zero lag for exact nonnegative; for rounding use maybe max of convolve if small?
    return float(2.0 * a.size * ss / (s*s))
```
But this ignores other lags rounding. For nonnegative exact, max zero lag. We'll use as screening.

`fast_conv_score(a)`:
```
def fast_conv_score(a):
    a=...
    s=sum...
    b=np.convolve(a,a)
    mb=float(np.max(b))
    return 2*n*mb/(s*s)
```
This matches evaluator except checks. Use for small n maybe to select. But repeated expensive? For n=1024 okay.

`candidate_from_base(base, rng, sigma)`:
```
def tiny_positive_candidate(base, rng, sigma):
    base = np.asarray(base, dtype=np.float64)
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, base.shape)
    cand = base * np.exp(noise)
    # ensure >0, finite
    cand = np.clip(cand, 1e-300, 1000.0)
    # maybe sometimes symmetrize? For nonnegative symmetric doesn't hurt exact but rounding maybe. Could keep.
    return [float(x) for x in cand]
```
For all positive, exp positive. No need negative.

`eval_candidate(seq)`:
```
def eval_candidate(seq):
    try:
        s=float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
    except Exception:
        return np.inf
    if not math.isfinite(s): return np.inf
    return s
```
Need `math.isfinite` for numpy? okay.

Let's implement `quick_score(a)`:
```
def quick_score(a):
    n=a.size
    s=float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01: return np.inf
    ss=float(np.dot(a,a))
    return float(2.0*n*ss/(s*s))
```
This is exact mathematical objective (for nonnegative, zero-lag) but not evaluator rounding. It is very fast O(n). For n=2048, 2048 ops. We can generate millions candidates. Then evaluate top candidates with `fast_conv_score` or `evaluate_sequence`. But if all quick scores >=2, the minimum quick score is very close to 2 for near uniform. `small_positive_perturbation`:
```
def make_perturbed(base, rng, sigma):
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, base.shape)
    cand = base * np.exp(noise)
    # avoid zero or inf
    cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
    # If any zero (unlikely), replace with tiny positive
    if cand.min() <= 0.0:
        cand = np.maximum(cand, np.finfo(np.float64).tiny)
    return cand
```

Let's test some mental examples. For sequence [a,b] with nonnegative: score = 4*(a^2+ab? Wait zero-lag a^2+b^2; sum^2=(a+b)^2). R=4(a^2+b^2)/(a+b)^2. Let t=a/b. R=4(t^2+1)/(t+1)^2. Minimum 2 at t=1. For t near 1, exact >2. Floating: a,b floats. Suppose a=1, b=1+eps. Conv via np.convolve: [1, 2+eps, 2+eps? Wait full [1, 1+eps, (1+eps)^2? Actually conv: [1, 2+eps, (1+eps)^2]? For [a,b]*[a,b] => [a^2, ab+ba=2ab, b^2], zero-lag is max( a^2, 2ab, b^2)? Wait full convolution of [a,b] with itself: index0 a^2, index1 a*b+b*a=2ab, index2 b^2. For nonnegative, is zero lag in this indexing? For n=2, zero lag index n-1=1, value 2ab, not a^2+b^2! Ah I made a critical indexing error! Let's recalc! For full convolution of a with itself: `np.convolve([a,b],[a,b])` = [a*a, a*b + b*a, b*b]. The zero-lag (shift 0) is at index n-1 = 1 and equals sum_i a_i^2? Wait for sequences length 2, zero-lag should a^2 + b^2 = a^2+b^2. But convolution index 1 is a*b + b*a = 2ab. That's lag 1, not zero? Let's align: Full convolution c_k = sum_i a_i b_{k-i} for k=0..2n-2. If b=a: c_0=a0*a0; c_1=a0*a1 + a1*a0 = 2a0a1; c_2=a1*a1. This is not autocorrelation! Ah! - Initialize q_i = 1 / ∑_k? Actually to satisfy constraints, first scale q small. We want max sum.
- Use primal-dual multiplicative weights (Lenstra)? Maintain q_i, y_k. Reduced cost for q_i: 1 - T ∑_k y_k p_{k-i}. Increase q_i if reduced cost >0. Increase y_k if constraint violated. This is classic primal-dual for LP. Since all coefficients nonnegative.
Algorithm (Kalev-Spielman?):
```
q_i small positive, y_k small positive.
for iterations:
  compute s_i = ∑_k y_k p_{k-i}
  update q_i *= exp(η (1 - T s_i) / something)  # variables with positive reduced cost grow
  compute v_k = (p*q)_k / T  # constraint violation (nonnegative)
  update y_k *= exp(η (v_k - 1)? or only violations)
```
At end scale q. This approximates LP. We can use FFT to compute s_i (correlation y,p) and v_k (convolution p,q). This is feasible. But tuning hard.

Primal-dual algorithm for max L1 with constraints b_k≤1:
- Maintain primal p≥0, dual y_k≥0.
- Lagrangian L = S(p) - ∑ y_k (b_k -1) (for max). Subgradient wrt p: 1 - (b derivative) y. If coefficient positive, increase p.
- Use multiplicative updates:
  p_i *= α * (1 - η (y * p)_i?) Wait derivative of b_k wrt p_i = 2 p_{k-i}. So gradient of constraint sum is 2 (y*p)_i. For maximization with y positive, increase p_i if 1 > 2η (y*p)_i.
  y_k *= β * max(0, b_k -1) (increase dual for violated constraints).
This is primal-dual. We can iterate:
```
Initialize p from sqrt profile normalized to max b=1 maybe.
y = zeros or indicator of peaks.
for it:
   # primal update
   s_i = correlation(y, p)  # ∑ y_k p_{k-i}
   # Reduced cost rc_i = 1 - lam * s_i
   logp += step_p * log(rc_i?) or * rc
   normalize p (scale max b to 1? or fix sum? We want maximize sum; maybe keep scale)
   # dual update
   b = p*p
   v = (b -1)/1
   logy += step_d * positive_log(y + v) etc.
```
Then scale p so max b=1, evaluate R=2n/S^2. If sum increases, R decreases.

Primal-dual algorithm for this LP:
Dual: minimize ∑ y_k s.t. s_i = (p*y)_i ≥1, y≥0.
Primal: max ∑ q_i s.t. t_k=(p*q)_k ≤1, q≥0.
Complementary at optimum: y_k>0 => t_k=1, s_i>1? y? For primal-dual multiplicative weights:
- Primal q_i *= exp(η (1 - s_i))? Since s_i constraints in primal? Let's derive standard.

For primal max c^T x s.t. A x ≤ b, x≥0; dual min b^T y s.t. A^T y ≥ c, y≥0.

for it:

  eps_p = 1 - s

```

Maybe implement the state-of-art LP via a simpler *alternating projections*:
Given r, we want u with large sum and r*u≤1. This is like finding a vector whose convolution with r is bounded. If we initialize u, iteratively:

- Where t>1, reduce u components contributing: u_i *= exp(-η (t>p)_i).
- Where t<1, increase u components? To maximize sum, we can allocate mass to positions that keep t low. A simple heuristic: u_i <- u_i * exp(η * (mean contribution slack?)).

Let's think of a more deterministic way to construct u. Constraint r*u≤1. For each k, the sum over i of r_{k-i} u_i ≤1. This is similar to scheduling: variables u_i have "weights" r over a window. To maximize sum, set u_i high where r is low. If r has high values at edges (sqrt profile), u may be high in middle? We can approximate by iterative water-filling: start u uniform. Violations t_k; for violated k, reduce all u_i contributing proportionally to r contribution. This is a multiplicative constraint projection:
```
u_i positive
for it:
   t = conv(r,u)
   excess = max(0, t-1)
   # gradient of excess: h_i = ∑ excess_k r_{k-i}
   u *= exp(-η h_i)
   # optionally normalize scale to increase until a constraint binds? Since objective max sum, after reductions, scale u up until max t=1.
   scale = 1 / max(t)
   u *= scale
```
This is if we only enforce constraints by reducing and then scaling up (increases sum). Starting u uniform, scale to max t=1. Then identify high t, reduce components causing them. Need compute objective fast with FFT. For nonnegative, max may be anywhere, no simple zero-lag. Use full convolution via FFT.

FFT convolution function:
```
def fft_convolve(a,b=None):
   if b is None: b=a
   n=len(a); m=len(b); N=1 << ((n+m-2).bit_length())
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N); fb=np.fft.rfft(b,N) if b is not a else fa
   out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fb,N)[:n+m-1]
   # roundoff can tiny negative; since p nonneg, set out=np.maximum(out,0)
   return out
```
For b=p, length 2n-1. Need N >= 2n-1. Actually for convolution length L=n+n-1, next pow2 >= L. For cross-correlation r*y with y length 2n-1, length n+(2n-1)-1=3n-2. Need separate size. For LP we use matrix not FFT; for custom updates maybe.

Objective function:
```
def sequence_score_fast(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,float)
    S=p.sum()
    if S<1e-12: return inf
    # scale not important but use normalized? Score = 2n max / S^2
    b=fft_convolve(p,p)
    mb=float(np.max(b))
    return float(2.0*p.size*mb/(S*S))
```
Because p nonnegative, b nonnegative. Rounding possible. To mimic official, for final maybe use np.convolve but FFT may differ tiny. We can at final call evaluate_sequence.

```
rows=[]; cols=[]; vals=[]
for i,r in enumerate(r):
   row_start=i; row_end=i+n
   rows.append(np.arange(row_start,row_end))
   cols.append(np.full(n,i))
   vals.append(np.full(n,r))
A = csr_matrix((np.concatenate(vals), (np.concatenate(rows), np.concatenate(cols))), shape=(2n-1,n))
```

Outer iteration for a length n:
```
def optimize_lp_length(n, seed, time_limit):
   p = initial_sqrt(n)
   best_p=p; best_score=score(p)
   # maybe try several initial profiles: sqrt, sqrt * (1+small random?), geometric?
   for profile in profiles:
       p0=...
       score0; if better set
   for outer in range(max_outer):
       if time deadline break
       u = lp_direction(p)
       if u None: break
       u = u / u.sum()
       # line search t in [0.2,0.5,0.1,0.8]? choose best p_t for small candidates
       best_t=0; best_pt=p; best_sc=score(p)
       for t in [0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9]:
           pt = (1-t)*p + t*u
           sc=score(pt)
           if sc < best_sc: ...
       if improved: p=best_t; best update
       else: break or reduce step
   return best_p,best_score
```

`objective_fft(a)`:
```
def objective_fft(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   n=a.size; S=float(a.sum())
   if not finite or S<0.01? For search any >0. Use scale invariant, return 2n max/(S^2)
   b=fft_conv_same(a)
   # due to roundoff, negative tiny; max positive
   mb=float(np.max(b))
   if mb<0: mb=0
   return float(2.0*n*mb/(S*S))
```

`make_sqrt_profile(n)`:
```
def make_sqrt_profile(n, seed=None):
   c=np.empty(n)
   if n>0:
      c[0]=1.0
      for i in range(1,n): c[i]=c[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(2*i)
   c /= c.sum()
   return c
```

```
def lp_direction(p):
   n=len(p)
   b=fft_conv_same(p); T=float(np.max(b)); if T<=0: return uniform
   r=p/T
   scale=float(np.max(r))
   if scale>0: r=r/scale
   # Try linprog
   if linprog is not None:
      try:
         A,bub = build_matrix(r)
         res=linprog(c=-np.ones(n), A_ub=A, b_ub=bub, bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
         if res.status in (0,? maybe 3?) and res.x is not None:
             u=np.asarray(res.x,float)
             u=np.maximum(u,0)
             s=u.sum()
             if s>1e-12: return u/s
      except Exception as e:
         print('LP failed', e)
   # fallback primal-dual
   return primal_dual_direction(r)
```

`primal_dual_direction(r)` as fallback. Need implement with limited iterations maybe 30 and FFT. Since r already scaled max 1. We need find u. We can use direct for n<=1000? But okay.
```
def primal_dual_direction(r, n_iters=30, eta=0.2):
   n=len(r); L=2n-1
   u=np.full(n, 1.0/n)
   y=np.full(L, 1.0/L)
   Ncorr=next_pow2(3*n-2); Nconv=next_pow2(2*n-1)
   Fr_corr=np.fft.rfft(r,Ncorr)
   Fr_conv=np.fft.rfft(r,Nconv)
   for it in range(n_iters):
       Fu=np.fft.rfft(u,Nconv)
       t=np.fft.irfft(Fr_conv*Fu, Nconv)[:L].real
       # normalize t to mean 1
       Ft=np.mean(t); if >0 t/=Ft
       s=np.fft.irfft(Fr_corr*np.conj(np.fft.rfft(y,Ncorr)), Ncorr)[:n].real
       # normalize s to mean 1
       Fs=np.mean(s); if>0 s/=Fs
       e_p = 1.0 - s
       e_y = t - 1.0
       # clip exponents
       u *= np.exp(np.clip(eta*(e_p - np.mean(e_p)), -1.0, 1.0))
       y *= np.exp(np.clip(eta*(e_y - np.mean(e_y)), -1.0, 1.0))
       u /= np.mean(u); y /= np.mean(y)
   u=np.maximum(u,0); s=u.sum(); if s>0: return u/s
```
This is rough. Could be used when LP unavailable.

- Primal variables x_j, dual y_i.
- `x_j <- x_j * exp(eta * (c_j - sum_i a_ij y_i))`

Let's implement `direction_primal_dual(p, iterations, eta)` that computes r from p (scaled to max1) and returns u normalized.
Detailed with normalization to maintain feasibility:
```
def direction_primal_dual(p, iters=40, eta=0.5):
    b=fft_conv_same(p); T=max(b)
    r=p/T
    scale=max(r)
    r/=scale
    n=len(r); L=2n-1
    u=np.ones(n)/n
    y=np.ones(L)/L
    Ncorr=next_pow2(3n-2); Nconv=next_pow2(2n-1)
    Fr_corr=fft(r,Ncorr); Fr_conv=fft(r,Nconv)
    for it in range(iters):
       Fu=fft(r,u,Nconv)
       t=irfft(Fr_conv*Fu,Nconv)[:L]
       # ensure nonnegative
       t=np.maximum(t,0)
       m_t=t.mean(); if m_t>0: t=t/m_t
       else: t=np.ones(L);
       m_y? y.
       s=irfft(Frac? correlation) # use r as A, y B
       s=np.maximum(s,0); m_s=s.mean(); s=s/m_s
       e_p=1-s; e_y=t-1
       # damp eta, maybe reduce if exps large
       maxe=max(max(abs(e_p)),max(abs(e_y)))
       step=min(eta, 1.0/maxe) if maxe>0 else eta
       u *= exp(clip(step*(e_p - mean(e_p)), -2,2))
       y *= exp(clip(step*(e_y - mean(e_y)), -2,2))
       # Normalize to average 1 and avoid underflow
       u /= u.mean(); y /= y.mean()
       u=np.maximum(u,1e-300); y=np.maximum(y,1e-300)
    u/=u.sum(); return u
```
But if we normalize t and s to mean 1 each iteration, the absolute b=1 condition is not enforced; however ratios okay. We can after direction obtain u, we don't need feasibility. The direction ratio is what matters. Good.

Detailed helper functions:

```
def next_pow2(m):
    if m <=1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(m)-1).bit_length()
```
Actually `(m).bit_length()`? For m=5, (m-1).bit_length()=2 ->4? Wait next power of 2 >=m: if m<=1 return1; return 1 << (m-1).bit_length(). For m=2 ->1<<1=2; m=3 ->1<<2=4.

```
def fft_convolution(self, a):
    n=a.size
    L=2*n-1
    N=next_pow2(L)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)[:L]
    out=np.maximum(out, 0.0) # due roundoff, but if true negative tiny; for nonneg clip. If roundoff makes max slightly negative? no.
    return out
```
Clipping negative to 0 for nonnegative p. But if p has negative due to numerical? We keep positive. Good. However, clipping may hide small negative artifact; official doesn't clip convolution, but nonnegative tiny negative won't be max. If all positive small and max underflows? no.

For correlations in PD, negative artifacts may occur; use maximum 0? s should be nonnegative because y,r nonnegative. Clip.

```
def fft_correlation(r, y): # r length n, y length >= n? returns length n s_i= sum_j r_j y_{i+j}
    n=r.size; L_y=y.size; N=next_pow2(n + L_y -1)
    Fr=np.fft.rfft(r,N); Fy=np.fft.rfft(y,N)
    out=np.fft.irfft(Fr * np.conj(Fy), N)[:n]
    return np.maximum(out,0.0)
```
Need y length 2n-1. Good.

```
def fast_score(p):
    p=np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n=p.size
    s=float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <=0: return inf
    b=fft_convolution(p)
    mb=float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(mb) or mb < 0: mb=0.0
    return float(2.0*n*mb/(s*s))
```
No need sum<0.01; scale invariant.

def to_plain_list(arr, scale=1.0):
    out=[]
    for x in arr:
       xf=float(x)*float(scale)
       if xf < 0 and xf > -1e-15: xf=0.0
       if xf < 0: xf=0.0
       # finite
       out.append(xf)
    return out
```

def insert_top(arr, score, top, limit=30):
    arr=np.asarray(arr,dtype=np.float64).copy()
    # remove nonfinite
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)) or arr.size==0: return
    # replace worst if top full
    if len(top) < limit:
       top.append([score, arr])
    else:
       worst_idx=0
       for i in range(len(top)):
          if top[i][0] > top[worst_idx][0]: worst_idx=i
       if score < top[worst_idx][0]: top[worst_idx]=[score,arr]
```

Initial profiles:
```
def profile_sqrt(n):
   a=np.empty(n); a[0]=1.0
   for i in range(1,n): a[i]=a[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(2*i)
   a /= a.sum()
   return a

def profile_pow(n, beta, alpha):
   i=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
   a=np.power(i+alpha, -beta)
   a /= a.sum(); return a

def profile_geom(n, ratio):
   a=np.power(ratio, np.arange(n,dtype=float))
   a/=a.sum(); return a

def profile_mixture(n): maybe 0.5 sqrt + 0.5 random? no.
```
For `profile_pow` beta=0.5 alpha maybe. Root series better, but can also include power.

Implement `select_top_k(top,k)`:
```
def top_k(arr_top,k):
   # copy list of indices
   idx=list(range(len(arr_top)))
   # selection sort first k: repeatedly find max score? We want lowest score.
   out=[]
   pool=list(arr_top)
   for _ in range(min(k,len(pool))):
      best_i=0
      for i in range(1,len(pool)):
         if pool[i][0] < pool[best_i][0]: best_i=i
      out.append(pool[best_i])
      pool.pop(best_i)
   return out
```

`as_normalized_arr`:
```
def as_normalized_arr(x):
   try:
      arr=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
   except Exception: return None
   if arr.ndim !=1 or arr.size==0: return None
   arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), np.maximum(arr,0.0), 0.0)
   s=float(arr.sum())
   if not math.isfinite(s) or s <=0: return None
   arr=arr/s
   return arr
```
Use math.isfinite.

`direction_lp` memory build:
```
def build_lp_matrix(r):
    n=r.size
    L=2*n-1
    # use csc if csc_matrix available
    if csc_matrix is not None:
       idx=np.arange(n,dtype=np.int64)
       data=np.empty(n*n, dtype=np.float64)
       indices=np.empty(n*n, dtype=np.int64)
       for i in range(n):
          start=i*n
          indices[start:start+n]=idx
          data[start:start+n]=r[i]
       indptr=np.arange(0,n*n+1,n,dtype=np.int64)
       A=csc_matrix((data,indices,indptr),shape=(L,n))
       return A
    else: dense ...
```
For n=2048, n*n=4,194,304, data 33.5MB, indices 33.5, indptr small. Okay.

for i in range(n): A[i:i+n,i]=r
return A
```

`direction_lp(p)`:

def direction_lp(p):
   p=np.asarray(p,float)
   n=p.size
   if n > 2048: return None
   b=fft_convolution(p); T=float(np.max(b))
   if T<=0 or not finite: return None
   r=p/T
   maxr=float(np.max(r))
   if maxr<=0: return None
   r=r/maxr
   if linprog is None: return None
   try:
      A=build_lp_matrix(r)
      c=-np.ones(n)
      b_ub=np.ones(2*n-1)
      bounds=[(0.0,None)]*n
      res=linprog(c,c? parameter name c, A_ub=A,b_ub=b_ub,bounds=bounds,method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
   except Exception as exc: print; return None
   if res is not None and hasattr(res,'x') and res.x is not None:
       if res.status == 0 or np.isfinite(res.x).all():
           u=np.asarray(res.x,dtype=np.float64)
           u=np.maximum(u,0.0)
           s=float(np.sum(u))
           if s>1e-12 and math.isfinite(s): return u/s
   return None
```
If res.status not 0 but x maybe optimal to tolerance? Could accept if status<2? I think 0 optimal. If status 1/2, no. But maybe due to presolve? Accept if finite and all constraints? Too much. Could check `res.fun` finite. We can accept status 0 only to avoid bad. If status 3 (limit?), not.

`direction_primal_dual(p, iterations=60, eta=0.5)`:
```
def direction_primal_dual(p, iterations=60, eta=0.5):
   p=np.asarray(p,float); n=p.size
   b=fft_convolution(p); T=float(np.max(b)); if T<=0: return profile_uniform(n)
   r=p/T; maxr=max(r); if maxr>0: r/=maxr
   L=2*n-1
   u=np.full(n,1.0/n)
   # scale u to make max conv <=1
   Nconv=next_pow2(2*n-1)
   Fr_conv=np.fft.rfft(r,Nconv)
   Fu=np.fft.rfft(u,Nconv)
   t=np.fft.irfft(Fr_conv*Fu,Nconv)[:L].real
   mx=float(np.max(t))
   if mx>0: u=u/mx
   y=np.full(L,1.0/L)
   Ncorr=next_pow2(3*n-2)
   Fr_corr=np.fft.rfft(r,Ncorr)
   s=np.fft.irfft(Fr_corr*np.conj(np.fft.rfft(y,Ncorr)),Ncorr)[:n].real
   ms=float(np.mean(s))
   if ms>0: y=y/ms
   for it in range(iterations):
       Fu=np.fft.rfft(u,Nconv)
       t=np.fft.irfft(Fr_conv*Fu,Nconv)[:L].real
       t=np.maximum(t,0.0)
       mt=float(np.mean(t))
       if mt>0: u=u/mt  # scale u; this doesn't change Fu? It will for next, but okay.
       s=np.fft.irfft(Fr_corr*np.conj(np.fft.rftp(y,Ncorr)),Ncorr)[:n].real
       s=np.maximum(s,0.0)
       ms=float(np.mean(s))
       if ms>0: y=y/ms
       ep=1.0-s
       ey=t-1.0
       # If correlation with scaled y gives mean 1, ep mean 0; t mean 1 after scaling u? But we scaled u before ep? ep uses old u? Actually ep should use current s (unaffected by u scaling). Good.
       # Normalize ep/ey to zero mean and unit scale
       ep=ep - np.mean(ep)
       ey=ey - np.mean(ey)
       scale_e = max(float(np.max(np.abs(ep))), float(np.max(np.abs(ey))), 1e-12)
       step = min(eta, 1.0/scale_e)
       epu = np.clip(step*ep, -1.0, 1.0)
       eyu = np.clip(step*ey, -1.0, 1.0)
       u = u * np.exp(epu)
       y = y * np.exp(eyu)
       # keep positive and normalize means
       u=np.maximum(u,1e-300); u/=float(np.mean(u))
       y=np.maximum(y,1e-300); y/=float(np.mean(y))
   u=np.maximum(u,0.0); s=float(np.sum(u))
   if s>1e-14: return u/s
   return profile_uniform(n)
```
But note inside loop, after scaling u by u/mt, we don't update Fu until next iter. We use t before scaling u for ey. If we scale u after computing t, then t mean becomes 1 for current but ey computed from pre-scaled t. Should compute t, normalize t itself for ey (not u). Let's simplify: don't scale u by mt; just normalize t for ey. Scaling u uniform irrelevant for ratios, but if we don't, t magnitude may drift; but t is normalized for ey. At end return u normalized. So remove `u=u/mt`, only `tn=t/mt` for ey. Similarly y.
```
       tn = t/mt if mt>0 else t
       sn = s/ms if ms>0 else s
       ep=1.0-sn; ey=tn-1.0
```
This maintains step ratios. Uniform scale of u/y not updated, but ratios only. Good.
Need `np.fft.rfft` typo fixed.

At end, u ratios may have very large dynamic range; normalize.

Does this PD actually find LP optimum? It approximates. We can increase iterations to 100. Learning rate adapted. Good.

`try_mixture(p,u)`:
```
def try_mixture(p,u):
    p=np.asarray(p,float); u=np.asarray(u,float)
    best_t=0.0; best_vec=p.copy(); best_score=fast_score(p.copy())
    for tval in [0.05,0.1,0.15,0.25,0.4,0.6,0.8,0.95]:
       v=(1.0-tval)*p + tval*u
       sm=float(np.sum(v))
       if sm<=0 or not math.isfinite(sm): continue
       v=v/sm
       sc=fast_score(v)
       if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
          best_score=sc; best_vec=v.copy(); best_t=tval
    return best_t,best_vec,best_score
```
We recompute base score each call; could pass. But okay? 8 FFT per direction, maybe expensive. We can use current score passed:
```
def try_mixture(p,u,current_score):
   best_score=current_score
   for tval...
```
If u==p, none. Good.

Random perturb:
```
def random_perturb(p,rng,sigma=0.1):
   v=p*np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,sigma,p.size))
   v=np.maximum(v,0.0)
   s=v.sum()
   if s>0: return v/s
   return p.copy()
```
Maybe try tail boost:
```
def tail_boost(p, alpha):
   # add a small reversed sqrt profile and renormalize
   left=profile_sqrt(p.size)
   right=np.reverse(left)
   v=(1-alpha)*p + alpha*right
   v/=v.sum(); return v
```
Could test in restarts.

`final_scales`:
```
def final_scales(arr):
    maxv=float(np.max(arr))
    scales=[]
    for s in (1.0,10.0,100.0,1000.0,10000.0):
       if maxv * s <= 1000.0:
          scales.append(s)
    # if can add a scale near clipping
    if maxv > 0:
       maxscale = 1000.0 / maxv
       if scales:
           last=scales[-1]
           if maxscale > last * 1.5:
              scales.append(min(maxscale, 10.0*last))
       else:
           scales.append(1.0)
    # ensure duplicates minimal
    out=[]
    for s in scales:
       ok=True
       for t in out:
          if abs(s-t) <= 1e-12*max(1.0,t): ok=False; break
       if ok: out.append(s)
    return out
```
If maxv small and scale 1e4, sum=1e4, square1e8, safe. For n=1 maxv=1, scales [1] maybe maxscale1000, append? if maxscale=1000 >1*1.5 -> append10, then maybe maxscale >10*1.5 -> append100, 1000? Actually loop will add up to 10000? If maxscale=1000, adds 10,100,1000? condition 1000>100*1.5 true add1000. Good. 5 scales. For n=6733 maxv~0.04, base scales all <=1000: 1,10,100,1000,10000 (maxv*10000=400), maxscale=25000, last10000 -> add20000? 400*? maxv*20000=800, okay. Maybe scale 20000, square4e8. Fine.

Let's review mathematical correctness of LP orientation. Our matrix A_{k,i}=r_{k-i} for i≤k<i+n. Convolution r*u: (r*u)_k =∑_i r_i u_{k-i}. Our column i values r_{k-i} with k-i from 0 to n-1. For column i, k=i..i+n-1, value r_{k-i}=r[0..n-1]. Yes.
Dual s_i =∑_k A_{k,i} y_k =∑_k r_{k-i} y_k, with k=i..i+n-1. Our correlation formula `s_i=∑_j r_j y_{i+j}` with j=0..n-1. This matches. Good.

PD update `u_i *= exp(step*(1 - normalized s_i))`. This matches primal reduced cost if s_i=∑ A y. Good. `y_k *= exp(step*(normalized t_k -1))`. Good.

If r max1, A entries <=1. Feasible u maybe small. Direction ratios okay.

```
def build_initial_profiles(n, rng):
   pro=[]
   left=profile_sqrt(n)
   pro.append(left)
   pro.append(profile_pow(n,0.5,1.0))
   pro.append(profile_pow(n,0.45,1.0))
   pro.append(profile_pow(n,0.55,1.0))
   pro.append(profile_geometric(n,0.5))
   right=left[::-1].copy()
   for w in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.3):
      a=(1.0-w)*left + w*right
      s=a.sum(); if s>0: pro.append(a/s)
   pro.append(profile_uniform(n))
   # random small perturbation of left
   a=left*np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.05,n))
   s=a.sum(); if s>0: pro.append(a/s)
   return pro
```

`fft_correlation`: Could be only in PD; implement:
```
def fft_correlation(r,y):
    r=np.asarray(r,float); y=np.asarray(y,float)
    n=r.size
    L=n + y.size -1
    N=next_pow2(L)
    Fr=np.fft.rfft(r,N); Fy=np.fft.rfft(y,N)
    out=np.fft.irfft(Fr*np.conj(Fy),N)[:n]
    return np.maximum(out,0.0)
```

def top_candidates_k(top,k):
    pool=list(top)
    out=[]
    for _ in range(min(k,len(pool))):
       best_i=0
       for i in range(1,len(pool)):
          if pool[i][0] < pool[best_i][0]: best_i=i
       out.append(pool[best_i])
       del pool[best_i]
    return out
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences with small peak normalized self-convolution.

The objective is

    R(a) = 2*n*max_k conv(a,a)_k / (sum a)^2.

We use several structural starts (truncated square roots of 1/(1-z), power
profiles, two-sided profiles), then iterate with an LP/primal-dual direction
similar in spirit to the previous state-of-the-art method.  For small sizes
an exact LP direction is used when SciPy is available; for larger sizes a fast
FFT primal-dual packing-LP approximation is used.  Candidates are kept by
rolling bests and finally validated with the official evaluator.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None

try:
    from scipy.sparse import csc_matrix
except Exception:
    csc_matrix = None


def next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def fft_convolution(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = next_pow2(L)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:L]
    return np.maximum(out, 0.0)


def fft_correlation(r, y):
    r = np.asarray(r, dtype=np.float64)
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64)
    n = r.size
    L = n + y.size - 1
    N = next_pow2(L)
    Fr = np.fft.rfft(r, N)
    Fy = np.fft.rfft(y, N)
    out = np.fft.irfft(Fr * np.conj(Fy), N)[:n]
    return np.maximum(out, 0.0)


def fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return float("inf")
    b = fft_convolution(a)
    if b.size == 0:
        mb = 0.0
    else:
        mb = float(np.max(b))
    if not math.isfinite(mb) or mb < 0.0:
        mb = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * mb / (s * s))


def official_score(seq):
    try:
        val = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")
    if not math.isfinite(val):
        return float("inf")
    return val


def as_normalized_arr(x):
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
        return None
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), np.maximum(arr, 0.0), 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    arr = arr / s
    return arr


def insert_top(arr, score, top, limit=25):
    arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
    if arr.ndim != 1 or arr.size == 0:
        return
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), np.maximum(arr, 0.0), 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(arr))
    if not math.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return
    arr = arr / s
    if not math.isfinite(score):
        score = fast_score(arr)
    if not math.isfinite(score):
        return
    if len(top) < limit:
        top.append([float(score), arr.copy()])
    else:
        worst = 0
        for i in range(1, len(top)):
            if top[i][0] > top[worst][0]:
                worst = i
        if score < top[worst][0]:
            top[worst] = [float(score), arr.copy()]


def top_candidates_k(top, k):
    pool = list(top)
    out = []
    limit = min(k, len(pool))
    for _ in range(limit):
        best_i = 0
        for i in range(1, len(pool)):
            if pool[i][0] < pool[best_i][0]:
                best_i = i
        out.append(pool[best_i])
        del pool[best_i]
    return out


def to_plain_list(arr, scale):
    out = []
    sc = float(scale)
    for x in arr:
        v = float(x) * sc
        if not math.isfinite(v) or v < 0.0:
            v = 0.0
        if v > 1000.0:
            v = 1000.0
        out.append(v)
    return out


def final_scales(arr):
    maxv = float(np.max(arr))
    scales = []
    base = (1.0, 10.0, 100.0, 1000.0, 10000.0)
    for s in base:
        if maxv * s <= 1000.0:
            scales.append(float(s))
    if maxv > 0.0:
        maxscale = 1000.0 / maxv
        if scales:
            last = scales[-1]
            if maxscale > last * 1.5:
                add = min(maxscale, 10.0 * last)
                scales.append(float(add))
        else:
            scales.append(1.0)
    out = []
    for s in scales:
        keep = True
        for t in out:
            if abs(s - t) <= 1e-12 * max(1.0, t):
                keep = False
                break
        if keep:
            out.append(s)
    if not out:
        out.append(1.0)
    return out


def profile_uniform(n):
    return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)


def profile_sqrt(n):
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[0] = 1.0
    for i in range(1, n):
        a[i] = a[i - 1] * (2 * i - 1) / (2 * i)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(s):
        return profile_uniform(n)
    return a / s


def profile_pow(n, beta, alpha):
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.power(i + max(0.05, float(alpha)), -float(beta))
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a) & (a > 0.0), a, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(s):
        return profile_uniform(n)
    return a / s


def profile_geometric(n, ratio):
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.power(float(ratio), i)
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(s):
        return profile_uniform(n)
    return a / s


def profile_two_sided(n, w):
    left = profile_sqrt(n)
    right = np.reverse(left).copy()
    a = (1.0 - float(w)) * left + float(w) * right
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(s):
        return left
    return a / s


def random_perturb(p, rng, sigma=0.1):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    v = p * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), p.size))
    v = np.maximum(v, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(v))
    if s <= 0.0 or not math.isfinite(s):
        return p.copy()
    return v / s


def build_initial_profiles(n, rng):
    out = []
    left = profile_sqrt(n)
    out.append(left)
    out.append(profile_pow(n, 0.5, 1.0))
    out.append(profile_pow(n, 0.45, 1.0))
    out.append(profile_pow(n, 0.55, 1.0))
    out.append(profile_pow(n, 0.5, 0.25))
    out.append(profile_geometric(n, 0.5))
    right = np.reverse(left).copy()
    for w in (0.03, 0.08, 0.15, 0.25):
        a = (1.0 - w) * left + w * right
        s = float(np.sum(a))
        if s > 0.0 and math.isfinite(s):
            out.append(a / s)
    a = left * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, n))
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s > 0.0 and math.isfinite(s):
        out.append(a / s)
    out.append(profile_uniform(n))
    return out


def build_lp_matrix(r):
    n = int(r.size)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if csc_matrix is not None:
        idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
        data = np.empty(n * n, dtype=np.float64)
        indices = np.empty(n * n, dtype=np.int64)
        for i in range(n):
            st = i * n
            indices[st:st + n] = idx
            data[st:st + n] = r[i]
        indptr = np.arange(0, n * n + 1, n, dtype=np.int64)
        return csc_matrix((data, indices, indptr), shape=(L, n))
    A = np.zeros((L, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(n):
        A[i:i + n, i] = r[i]
    return A


def direction_lp(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n <= 0 or n > 1024 or linprog is None:
        return None
    b = fft_convolution(p)
    T = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    if not math.isfinite(T) or T <= 0.0:
        return None
    r = p / T
    maxr = float(np.max(r))
    if not math.isfinite(maxr) or maxr <= 0.0:
        return None
    r = r / maxr
    try:
        A = build_lp_matrix(r)
        c_obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        b_ub = np.ones(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n
        res = linprog(
            c_obj,
            A_ub=A,
            b_ub=b_ub,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options={"presolve": True},
        )
    except Exception:
        return None

    if res is not None and getattr(res, "x", None) is not None:
        ok_status = getattr(res, "success", False) or getattr(res, "status", 1) == 0
        if ok_status:
            u = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            u = np.where(np.isfinite(u), np.maximum(u, 0.0), 0.0)
            s = float(np.sum(u))
            if math.isfinite(s) and s > 1e-14:
                return u / s
    return None


def direction_primal_dual(p, iterations=80, eta=0.5):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    b = fft_convolution(p)
    T = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    if not math.isfinite(T) or T <= 0.0:
        return profile_uniform(n)
    r = p / T
    maxr = float(np.max(r))
    if not math.isfinite(maxr) or maxr <= 0.0:
        return profile_uniform(n)
    r = r / maxr

    L = 2 * n - 1
    u = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    y = np.full(L, 1.0 / float(L), dtype=np.float64)

    Nconv = next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    Ncorr = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    Fr_conv = np.fft.rfft(r, Nconv)
    Fr_corr = np.fft.rfft(r, Ncorr)

    iterations = max(8, int(iterations))
    eta = float(eta)

    for _ in range(iterations):
        Fu = np.fft.rfft(u, Nconv)
        t = np.fft.irfft(Fr_conv * Fu, Nconv)[:L].real
        t = np.maximum(t, 0.0)
        mt = float(np.mean(t))
        if mt > 0.0 and math.isfinite(mt):
            tn = t / mt
        else:
            tn = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64)

        Fy = np.fft.rfft(y, Ncorr)
        s = np.fft.irfft(Fr_corr * np.conj(Fy), Ncorr)[:n].real
        s = np.maximum(s, 0.0)
        ms = float(np.mean(s))
        if ms > 0.0 and math.isfinite(ms):
            sn = s / ms
        else:
            sn = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)

        ep = 1.0 - sn
        ey = tn - 1.0
        ep = ep - float(np.mean(ep))
        ey = ey - float(np.mean(ey))

        maxe = max(float(np.max(np.abs(ep))), float(np.max(np.abs(ey))), 1e-12)
        step = min(eta, 1.0 / maxe)

        epu = np.clip(step * ep, -1.0, 1.0)
        eyu = np.clip(step * ey, -1.0, 1.0)

        u = u * np.exp(epu)
        y = y * np.exp(eyu)

        u = np.maximum(u, 1e-300)
        y = np.maximum(y, 1e-300)

        mu = float(np.mean(u))
        my = float(np.mean(y))
        if mu > 0.0 and math.isfinite(mu):
            u = u / mu
        if my > 0.0 and math.isfinite(my):
            y = y / my

    u = np.where(np.isfinite(u), np.maximum(u, 0.0), 0.0)
    su = float(np.sum(u))
    if math.isfinite(su) and su > 1e-14:
        return u / su
    return profile_uniform(n)


def try_mixture(p, u, current_score):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    u = np.asarray(u, dtype=np.float64)
    best_t = 0.0
    best_vec = p.copy()
    best_score = float(current_score)
    if u.size != p.size or not np.all(np.isfinite(u)):
        return best_t, best_vec, best_score

    t_values = (0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.50, 0.70, 0.90, 0.99)
    for t in t_values:
        v = (1.0 - float(t)) * p + float(t) * u
        sv = float(np.sum(v))
        if not math.isfinite(sv) or sv <= 0.0:
            continue
        v = v / sv
        sc = fast_score(v)
        if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_vec = v.copy()
            best_t = float(t)
    return best_t, best_vec, best_score


def optimize_length(n, phase_until, top, rng, allow_lp):
    if n <= 0:
        return
    n = int(n)
    best_arr = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    profiles = build_initial_profiles(n, rng)
    for prof in profiles:
        if time.monotonic() >= phase_until:
            break
        if prof is None:
            continue
        sc = fast_score(prof)
        if math.isfinite(sc):
            insert_top(prof, sc, top)
            if sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_arr = prof.copy()

    if best_arr is None:
        best_arr = profile_uniform(n)
        best_score = fast_score(best_arr)
        insert_top(best_arr, best_score, top)

    p = best_arr.copy()
    p_score = float(best_score)
    no_improve = 0
    outer = 0
    lp_used = 0
    max_no_improve = 35

    while time.monotonic() < phase_until and no_improve < max_no_improve:
        outer += 1
        now = time.monotonic()
        remaining = phase_until - now
        if remaining < 3.0:
            break

        direction = None
        if allow_lp and lp_used < 20 and remaining > 45.0:
            direction = direction_lp(p)
            if direction is not None:
                lp_used += 1

        if direction is None and remaining > 8.0:
            if remaining > 180.0:
                iters = 90
            elif remaining > 60.0:
                iters = 60
            else:
                iters = 25
            direction = direction_primal_dual(p, iterations=iters, eta=0.5)

        improved = False
        if direction is not None:
            diff = float(np.sum((direction - p) ** 2))
            if diff > 1e-18:
                t, v, sc = try_mixture(p, direction, p_score)
                if t > 0.0 and math.isfinite(sc) and sc < p_score - 1e-13:
                    p = v
                    p_score = sc
                    improved = True
                    insert_top(p, p_score, top)
                    if outer <= 3 or outer % 10 == 0 or sc < 1.6:
                        print("n=%d outer=%d score=%.10f" % (n, outer, sc))

        if not improved:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve in (5, 10, 18):
                restarts = [
                    random_perturb(p, rng, 0.10),
                    random_perturb(p, rng, 0.25),
                    profile_two_sided(n, 0.05),
                    profile_two_sided(n, 0.15),
                    profile_two_sided(n, 0.30),
                ]
                for rd in restarts:
                    if time.monotonic() >= phase_until:
                        break
                    if rd is None:
                        continue
                    t, v, sc = try_mixture(p, rd, p_score)
                    if t > 0.0 and math.isfinite(sc) and sc < p_score - 1e-13:
                        p = v
                        p_score = sc
                        improved = True
                        no_improve = 0
                        insert_top(p, p_score, top)
                        break

    insert_top(p, p_score, top)
    print("phase n=%d best_fast=%.10f" % (n, p_score))


def choose_final(top, budget_s, t0):
    now = time.monotonic()
    remaining = max(5.0, float(budget_s) - (now - t0) - 3.0)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + min(remaining, 240.0)

    best_seq = [1.0]
    best_score = official_score(best_seq)

    # A couple of safe bare nonnegative fallbacks.
    fallbacks = [[1.0], [0.5, 0.5], [1.0, 0.5]]
    for seq in fallbacks:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0:
            break
        sc = official_score(seq)
        if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_seq = list(seq)

    if not top:
        return best_seq

    k = 12
    cands = top_candidates_k(top, k)
    stop = False

    for cand in cands:
        if stop:
            break
        arr = cand[1]
        scales = final_scales(arr)
        for scale in scales:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 1.0:
                stop = True
                break
            seq = to_plain_list(arr, scale)
            sc = official_score(seq)
            if math.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_seq = list(seq)
                print("final improved to %.10f" % best_score)

    # Make sure the returned object is a plain list of finite floats.
    out = []
    for x in best_seq:
        try:
            y = float(x)
        except Exception:
            y = 0.0
        if not math.isfinite(y) or y < 0.0:
            y = 0.0
        if y > 1000.0:
            y = 1000.0
        out.append(y)

    if not out:
        out = [1.0]
    s = sum(out)
    if s < 0.01:
        scale = 1.0 / max(float(len(out)), 1.0)
        out = [x * scale for x in out]
        s = sum(out)

    if s < 0.01:
        out = [1.0]

    return out


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(1.0, float(budget_s))

    if budget < 30.0:
        final_reserve = max(3.0, 0.20 * budget)
    else:
        final_reserve = max(60.0, min(240.0, 0.12 * budget))
    work_total = max(5.0, budget - final_reserve)

    top = []
    base = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    insert_top(base, fast_score(base), top)

    try:
        parent = height_sequence_1
    except NameError:
        parent = None
    if parent is not None:
        pa = as_normalized_arr(parent)
        if pa is not None and pa.size <= 30000:
            sc = fast_score(pa)
            if math.isfinite(sc):
                insert_top(pa, sc, top)

    # Absolute phase deadlines allow earlier phases to give their leftover time
    # to later phases.
    phases = [
        (1024, 0.08),
        (2048, 0.18),
        (4096, 0.24),
        (6733, 0.50),
    ]

    cum = 0.0
    absolute_deadline = t0 + work_total

    for n, frac in phases:
        cum += float(frac)
        phase_until = min(absolute_deadline, t0 + work_total * cum)
        if time.monotonic() >= phase_until:
            continue

        allow_lp = (n <= 1024) and (linprog is not None)
        optimize_length(n, phase_until, top, rng, allow_lp)

    final_seq = choose_final(top, budget_s, t0)

    # One last official sanity check, unless time is absurdly short.
    if time.monotonic() < t0 + budget - 2.0:
        sc = official_score(final_seq)
        if not math.isfinite(sc):
            final_seq = [1.0]

    return final_seq
```
